Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `mult_add_int_18x19` (precision INT19)
- computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
- input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
- output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: on `clk`, the block registers its operands (`ax`, `ay`, `bx`) at one clock edge and `result` at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in `result` after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. `chainin` is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches `result` 2 cycles after it is applied. `chainout` is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value `result` loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before `result` shows it.
- reset: `reset` is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
- power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where `reset` is low, so, for a block whose inputs, `chainin` included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
- tie-off: `chainin` = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's `chainout` or a constant may drive it); `scanin` = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: `ay` is driven directly, and `scanout` is left unconnected); `mode_sigs` = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. `chainout` reaches only the next block's `chainin`, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, stereovision1.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

Baseline: the unmodified stereovision1 under this run's VPR settings (channel width 300, median of five seeds, 1 to 5) has a worst register-to-register path of 5.47292 ns on its one clock tm3_clk_v0 (I/O pads not timed) and uses 42 DSP tiles (the same at each of the five seeds). Its used logic-block area is 2.56155e+07 minimum-width transistor areas, so its area × delay, the objective, is 1.40192e+08. Its slowest stage (read off the seed-1 timing report) is in corr: one hard multiply on a DSP tile (mult_9x9_fixed_pt) straight into a flip-flop (the product register), no adder and no LUT; wire 1.73 ns into the multiply, 1.45 out; 5.429 ns on that report. The next slowest stage on the same report that starts at another register, in corr (another product register, endpoint lo09773, not path 1's lo09847), is the same shape as path 1, one hard multiply on a DSP tile straight into a flip-flop, 5.427 ns, so a rewrite that shortens only the slowest stage leaves the period at about that; one that also shortens this stage can take it lower. Any path your rewrite creates is compared against that critical path.

Wire and adder delays measured on the unmodified design's own placement under this run's VPR settings, with the design's hard multiplier standing in for the block, given as a range with the median in parentheses. The block's own stage, input register to output register, 2.219 ns, is below the baseline's critical path.

Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, what it already is in the baseline: on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, four to a tile on 35 of the 42 tiles.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 4 products in one tree of adders.

idinstances
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64
corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)64

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Cost (guidance: go against it only with a reason in the plan)
  • The clock period is the delay of the slowest stage (the logic between one register and the next). A rewrite that does not touch the slowest stage should not be expected to make the design faster, and should not make another stage slower than it.
  • Moving or adding registers changes stage lengths and when values arrive.
  • Area falls when moving soft adders and flip-flops into blocks frees whole CLBs; a DSP tile costs the area of about 9 CLBs.
  • Synthesis may already have placed the design's multiplies on tiles, so a block that only replaces one saves little: count tiles against the baseline.
Objective

Minimize area × delay against the original design. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or none expected to lower area × delay, or the best result so far is the best available. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

response

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)
SHARED_REDUCTION: corr_out_tmp of each corr / corr_seq instance (the adder at stereovision1.v:1385 and :1869 that joins the two products of one instance); the further join of the p and n halves is the soft adder in sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem at :316/:368/:400 and stays soft
BLOCKS: 152
TILES: 152
STAGE_NS: 2.35
MAPPING: per corr instance (64x) and per corr_seq instance (12x), two blocks chained, A/B = the "re" pair, C/D = the "im" pair.
block1 ("re" product, line 1374 / 1867): clk=tm3_clk_v0 (the module's clk port), reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0, A_nxt}, ay={11'b0, B_nxt}, bx=36'b0, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0, result=unconnected, chainout -> block2.chainin, scanout=unconnected.
in corr: A_nxt = the D input of the sh_reg register that produces in_l_re (wen ? d_l_1 : out_01 for instance 0, wen ? out_{k-1}1 : out_k1 for instance k), B_nxt = the D input of the sh_reg register that produces in_r_re (wen ? d_r_1 : out_r1), shared by all corr instances of that wrapper.
in corr_seq: A_nxt = (new_data ? in_l_re : in_l_re_reg), B_nxt = (new_data ? in_r_re : in_r_re_reg).
block2 ("im" product, line 1375 / 1868): clk=same clk, reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0, C}, ay={11'b0, D}, bx=36'b0, chainin=block1.chainout, scanin=19'b0, result=res (64 b), chainout=unconnected, scanout=unconnected.
in corr: C = in_l_im (out_k2), D = in_r_im (out_r2), both exactly as today.
in corr_seq: C = in_l_im_reg, D = in_r_im_reg, both exactly as today.
the sum: res[15:0] replaces corr_out_tmp; corr_out stays a soft register: always @(posedge clk) if (new_data) corr_out <= res[15:0];
PLAN:

What is converted and why all four regions together

All four candidate regions are converted; none is left. They are the same arithmetic in two modules: each corr (64 instances) and each corr_seq (12 instances) computes lrexrre_reg = l_re*r_re, limxrim_reg = l_im*r_im, corr_out_tmp = lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg. The baseline's two slowest stages are both of this shape (a 9x9 on a DSP tile straight into a product register, 5.429 / 5.427 ns), and all 152 products are that same shape, so converting a subset leaves a ~5.43 ns stage and buys nothing. The rewrite is therefore all-or-nothing, and it is taken all.

Structure of the rewrite (per corr / corr_seq instance)

Two blocks in a chain replace both products and their adder:

  • block1 computes A*B and exports it on chainout only (its result is unused);
  • block2 computes C*D + chainin, so block2.result = A*B + C*D, which is corr_out_tmp.

Width/sign: all four operands are 8-bit unsigned (input[7:0], no signed), zero-extended into ax/ay; each product fits in 16 bits and the original 16-bit corr_out_tmp wraps mod 2^16, so result[15:0] is bit-identical to the original sum. Every term of the original arithmetic is kept; bx is the only unused port and is tied to 0, as the design has no third addend.

Cycle alignment — why one operand pair is taken one tick early

Writing x(t) for the value during cycle t, the original gives corr_out_tmp(t) = A(t-2)B(t-2) + C(t-2)D(t-2). The chained pair gives result2(t) = ax2(t-2)ay2(t-2) + chainin(t-2) with chainin(t) = chainout1(t) = ax1(t-1)ay1(t-1), i.e. result2(t) = C(t-2)D(t-2) + ax1(t-3)ay1(t-3). Feeding block1 with A_nxt, the D input of the register that holds A (A(t) = A_nxt(t-1)), makes ax1(t-3) = A(t-2) and the two expressions identical, cycle for cycle, with no assumption about wen/new_data. That node already exists: it is the enable mux in front of the sh_reg register (for corr) or in front of in_l_re_reg/in_r_re_reg (for corr_seq), so nothing new is computed, only tapped. There is no ASSUMES: line; the rewrite is exactly equivalent on any stimulus.

corr_out keeps its enable (new_data) and stays a soft register — the block has no clock enable, so no enabled register of the design is moved into a block.

Reset

reset is tied to 1'b0 on all 152 blocks. The registers the blocks replace (lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg, corr_out_tmp) are free-running in the original and are not cleared by the design's rst (rst only reaches port_bus_2to1_1's counter_out_tmp/bus_word_*_tmp, :937-952, port_bus_1to0 and the dividers). Driving the blocks' reset from rst would clear them while the reference keeps counting, and the testbench's mid-run resets (reset_pattern, random rst at 1/64) would then diverge on the first wen after a release; tying it low reproduces the original after every reset. Power-up: with reset low each block loads every edge, so block1's result/chainout are defined from edge 1 and block2's result from edge 3. That X window cannot be captured: corr_out only loads when vidin_new_data_scld_1_2to3_left (etc.) is 1, and that signal is a register of counter_out_tmp, which rst holds at 0 through the testbench's 4 reset cycles, so the earliest load is well after cycle 3. Nothing relies on the initial value of any added register (none is added).

Blocks, tiles, area

152 blocks (2 per converted instance x 76 instances), each alone on a DSP tile: the design then uses 152 DSP tiles of the 306 available, against 42 in the baseline (the 152 mult_9x9_fixed_pt products, four to a tile, all disappear). DSP area goes 1.066e7 -> 3.857e7. Against that, 48 flip-flops (lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg, corr_out_tmp) and one 16-bit adder leave soft logic per instance — 3648 FFs and ~1216 LUTs, roughly a third of the design's ~536 CLBs, about 4.9e6 of CLB area. Estimated new area ~4.86e7, i.e. about 1.9x the baseline; break-even on area x delay is therefore a period of ~2.89 ns against the baseline's 5.47292. This is the cost the plan deliberately accepts: the block is 4x less area-efficient per multiply than the fractured 9x9, so the rewrite only pays if it roughly halves the period, which it does for every stage it touches.

Stages

  • Longest series of blocks in one stage: 2 (block1's input register -> chainout -> block2's chainin register), delay 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns — the dedicated chain costs exactly the same as a block's own stage, so chaining is free.
  • Block internal stage (input registers -> result): 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns.
  • Into a block: soft FF 0.060 + wire 1.417-1.940 (median 1.732) + setup 0.019 = 1.81-2.02 ns; for block1 the advance mux adds one LUT on that path, ~2.3-2.4 ns — this is the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, ~2.35 ns.
  • Out of a block: result clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire 0.933-1.729 (median 1.314) + the new_data enable mux + FF setup, ~1.5-2.0 ns.

Soft logic leaving the slowest stage: the baseline's slowest stage and its twin (both register -> wire 1.73 -> mult_9x9 -> wire 1.45 -> product FF, 5.429 / 5.427 ns) are removed outright — the multiply now sits between two registers inside the block and the two wires become separate stages. The 16-bit adder and its register (corr_out_tmp) also leave their stage, which was not the slowest. Nothing is added to any stage except the one LUT of the advance mux noted above.

Caveat stated plainly: the two slowest stages on the seed-1 report are both removed, and the report gives nothing below them, so the resulting period is set by untouched soft stages I cannot read off what I was given — the likely candidates are the 18-bit corr_out_1X/2X/4X adders in sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem (:316, :368, :400) and the 17-bit adder in wrapper_norm (:1445). If those sit near 2.4-2.9 ns the rewrite wins by up to ~19%; above ~2.9 ns it loses. That is the measurement this candidate is worth making.

Register stages, operands to result, for each converted region

  • corr.$mul$...:1374$78 and ...:1375$79: before — operands (out_k1/out_k2/out_r1/out_r2) -> 1 stage -> product register -> 1 more stage -> corr_out_tmp: 2 stages to the sum. After — block1 operands are the same values taken one shift-tick early, then block1 input registers (1) -> block2 chainin register (2) -> block2 result (3): 3 block stages, but the one-tick-early tap cancels one, so the sum still lands exactly 2 stages after the design's own registered operands, on the same cycle as corr_out_tmp did. corr_out is unchanged, 1 further enabled stage.
  • corr_seq.$mul$...:1867$104 and ...:1868$105: identical, with in_l_re_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_im_reg kept as the design's enabled input registers (they are still read by the advance mux and by block2), products and corr_out_tmp removed, latency to corr_out unchanged.

Edit list

  1. corr (:1352-1387): keep the 7-port interface and the corr_out register; the ports now carrying in_l_re/in_r_re receive the advanced values (rename to in_l_re_nxt/in_r_re_nxt). Delete lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg, corr_out_tmp and the always-block arithmetic; instantiate the two chained blocks as in MAPPING; always @(posedge clk) if (new_data) corr_out <= res[15:0];
  2. sh_reg (:1326-1351): add a trailing output dnxt_1 with assign dnxt_1 = wen ? din_1 : dout_1; (a tap on the mux that already feeds dout_1, so no new logic) and extend every positional instantiation in wrapper_corr_20, wrapper_corr_10, wrapper_corr_5_seq with a wire for it (unused in wrapper_corr_5_seq).
  3. wrapper_corr_20 (:1247-1267) and wrapper_corr_10 (:1590-1610): pass inst_sh_reg_k.dnxt_1 and inst_sh_reg_r_1.dnxt_1 in the in_l_re/in_r_re positions of each corr instance; in_l_im/in_r_im keep out_k2/out_r2.
  4. corr_seq (:1829-1871): keep the four input registers and corr_out; delete lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg, corr_out_tmp; instantiate the two chained blocks with ax/ay of block1 driven by new_data ? in_l_re : in_l_re_reg and new_data ? in_r_re : in_r_re_reg.
  5. wrapper_corr_5_seq and everything above corr/corr_seq (the wrappers' corr_out_k registers, the top's 18-bit p+n adders, port_bus_*, the dividers) are untouched.
  6. Do not add a definition of mult_add_int_18x19 to stereovision1.v; instantiate the primitive only.

Read completed /work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v
input
{
 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v"

output
1	// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
6	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
7	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
8	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
9	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
10	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
11	// inputs).
12	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
13	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
14	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
15	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
16	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
17	// X until the first clear.
18	module mult_add_int_18x19 (
19	    input clk,
20	    input reset,
21	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
22	    input [17:0] ax,
23	    input [18:0] ay,
24	    input [35:0] bx,
25	    input [63:0] chainin,
26	    input [18:0] scanin,
27	    output reg [63:0] result,
28	    output [63:0] chainout,
29	    output [18:0] scanout
30	);
31	
32	    // stage 1: the input registers
33	    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
34	    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
35	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
36	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
37	
38	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
39	    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
40	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
41	
42	    always @(posedge clk) begin
43	        if (reset) begin
44	            result <= 0;
45	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
46	        end
47	        else begin
48	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
49	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
50	        end
51	    end
52	
53	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
54	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
55	
56	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019

out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is stereovision1.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, what it already is in the baseline: on a DSP tile in mult_9x9_fixed_pt mode, four to a tile on 35 of the 42 tiles.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 4 products in one tree of adders.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds, and how many DSP tiles the design then uses.
  • The longest series of blocks in any stage and its delay, against the slowest stage.
  • What soft logic leaves which stage, and whether that stage is the slowest.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)
SHARED_REDUCTION: corr_out_tmp of each corr / corr_seq instance (the adder at stereovision1.v:1385 and :1869 that joins the two products of one instance); the further join of the p and n halves is the soft adder in sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem at :316/:368/:400 and stays soft
BLOCKS: 152
TILES: 152
STAGE_NS: 2.35
MAPPING: per corr instance (64x) and per corr_seq instance (12x), two blocks chained, A/B = the "re" pair, C/D = the "im" pair.
block1 ("re" product, line 1374 / 1867): clk=tm3_clk_v0 (the module's clk port), reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0, A_nxt}, ay={11'b0, B_nxt}, bx=36'b0, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0, result=unconnected, chainout -> block2.chainin, scanout=unconnected.
in corr: A_nxt = the D input of the sh_reg register that produces in_l_re (wen ? d_l_1 : out_01 for instance 0, wen ? out_{k-1}1 : out_k1 for instance k), B_nxt = the D input of the sh_reg register that produces in_r_re (wen ? d_r_1 : out_r1), shared by all corr instances of that wrapper.
in corr_seq: A_nxt = (new_data ? in_l_re : in_l_re_reg), B_nxt = (new_data ? in_r_re : in_r_re_reg).
block2 ("im" product, line 1375 / 1868): clk=same clk, reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0, C}, ay={11'b0, D}, bx=36'b0, chainin=block1.chainout, scanin=19'b0, result=res (64 b), chainout=unconnected, scanout=unconnected.
in corr: C = in_l_im (out_k2), D = in_r_im (out_r2), both exactly as today.
in corr_seq: C = in_l_im_reg, D = in_r_im_reg, both exactly as today.
the sum: res[15:0] replaces corr_out_tmp; corr_out stays a soft register: always @(posedge clk) if (new_data) corr_out <= res[15:0];
PLAN:

What is converted and why all four regions together

All four candidate regions are converted; none is left. They are the same arithmetic in two modules: each corr (64 instances) and each corr_seq (12 instances) computes lrexrre_reg = l_re*r_re, limxrim_reg = l_im*r_im, corr_out_tmp = lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg. The baseline's two slowest stages are both of this shape (a 9x9 on a DSP tile straight into a product register, 5.429 / 5.427 ns), and all 152 products are that same shape, so converting a subset leaves a ~5.43 ns stage and buys nothing. The rewrite is therefore all-or-nothing, and it is taken all.

Structure of the rewrite (per corr / corr_seq instance)

Two blocks in a chain replace both products and their adder:

  • block1 computes A*B and exports it on chainout only (its result is unused);
  • block2 computes C*D + chainin, so block2.result = A*B + C*D, which is corr_out_tmp.

Width/sign: all four operands are 8-bit unsigned (input[7:0], no signed), zero-extended into ax/ay; each product fits in 16 bits and the original 16-bit corr_out_tmp wraps mod 2^16, so result[15:0] is bit-identical to the original sum. Every term of the original arithmetic is kept; bx is the only unused port and is tied to 0, as the design has no third addend.

Cycle alignment — why one operand pair is taken one tick early

Writing x(t) for the value during cycle t, the original gives corr_out_tmp(t) = A(t-2)B(t-2) + C(t-2)D(t-2). The chained pair gives result2(t) = ax2(t-2)ay2(t-2) + chainin(t-2) with chainin(t) = chainout1(t) = ax1(t-1)ay1(t-1), i.e. result2(t) = C(t-2)D(t-2) + ax1(t-3)ay1(t-3). Feeding block1 with A_nxt, the D input of the register that holds A (A(t) = A_nxt(t-1)), makes ax1(t-3) = A(t-2) and the two expressions identical, cycle for cycle, with no assumption about wen/new_data. That node already exists: it is the enable mux in front of the sh_reg register (for corr) or in front of in_l_re_reg/in_r_re_reg (for corr_seq), so nothing new is computed, only tapped. There is no ASSUMES: line; the rewrite is exactly equivalent on any stimulus.

corr_out keeps its enable (new_data) and stays a soft register — the block has no clock enable, so no enabled register of the design is moved into a block.

Reset

reset is tied to 1'b0 on all 152 blocks. The registers the blocks replace (lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg, corr_out_tmp) are free-running in the original and are not cleared by the design's rst (rst only reaches port_bus_2to1_1's counter_out_tmp/bus_word_*_tmp, :937-952, port_bus_1to0 and the dividers). Driving the blocks' reset from rst would clear them while the reference keeps counting, and the testbench's mid-run resets (reset_pattern, random rst at 1/64) would then diverge on the first wen after a release; tying it low reproduces the original after every reset. Power-up: with reset low each block loads every edge, so block1's result/chainout are defined from edge 1 and block2's result from edge 3. That X window cannot be captured: corr_out only loads when vidin_new_data_scld_1_2to3_left (etc.) is 1, and that signal is a register of counter_out_tmp, which rst holds at 0 through the testbench's 4 reset cycles, so the earliest load is well after cycle 3. Nothing relies on the initial value of any added register (none is added).

Blocks, tiles, area

152 blocks (2 per converted instance x 76 instances), each alone on a DSP tile: the design then uses 152 DSP tiles of the 306 available, against 42 in the baseline (the 152 mult_9x9_fixed_pt products, four to a tile, all disappear). DSP area goes 1.066e7 -> 3.857e7. Against that, 48 flip-flops (lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg, corr_out_tmp) and one 16-bit adder leave soft logic per instance — 3648 FFs and ~1216 LUTs, roughly a third of the design's ~536 CLBs, about 4.9e6 of CLB area. Estimated new area ~4.86e7, i.e. about 1.9x the baseline; break-even on area x delay is therefore a period of ~2.89 ns against the baseline's 5.47292. This is the cost the plan deliberately accepts: the block is 4x less area-efficient per multiply than the fractured 9x9, so the rewrite only pays if it roughly halves the period, which it does for every stage it touches.

Stages

  • Longest series of blocks in one stage: 2 (block1's input register -> chainout -> block2's chainin register), delay 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns — the dedicated chain costs exactly the same as a block's own stage, so chaining is free.
  • Block internal stage (input registers -> result): 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns.
  • Into a block: soft FF 0.060 + wire 1.417-1.940 (median 1.732) + setup 0.019 = 1.81-2.02 ns; for block1 the advance mux adds one LUT on that path, ~2.3-2.4 ns — this is the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, ~2.35 ns.
  • Out of a block: result clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire 0.933-1.729 (median 1.314) + the new_data enable mux + FF setup, ~1.5-2.0 ns.

Soft logic leaving the slowest stage: the baseline's slowest stage and its twin (both register -> wire 1.73 -> mult_9x9 -> wire 1.45 -> product FF, 5.429 / 5.427 ns) are removed outright — the multiply now sits between two registers inside the block and the two wires become separate stages. The 16-bit adder and its register (corr_out_tmp) also leave their stage, which was not the slowest. Nothing is added to any stage except the one LUT of the advance mux noted above.

Caveat stated plainly: the two slowest stages on the seed-1 report are both removed, and the report gives nothing below them, so the resulting period is set by untouched soft stages I cannot read off what I was given — the likely candidates are the 18-bit corr_out_1X/2X/4X adders in sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem (:316, :368, :400) and the 17-bit adder in wrapper_norm (:1445). If those sit near 2.4-2.9 ns the rewrite wins by up to ~19%; above ~2.9 ns it loses. That is the measurement this candidate is worth making.

Register stages, operands to result, for each converted region

  • corr.$mul$...:1374$78 and ...:1375$79: before — operands (out_k1/out_k2/out_r1/out_r2) -> 1 stage -> product register -> 1 more stage -> corr_out_tmp: 2 stages to the sum. After — block1 operands are the same values taken one shift-tick early, then block1 input registers (1) -> block2 chainin register (2) -> block2 result (3): 3 block stages, but the one-tick-early tap cancels one, so the sum still lands exactly 2 stages after the design's own registered operands, on the same cycle as corr_out_tmp did. corr_out is unchanged, 1 further enabled stage.
  • corr_seq.$mul$...:1867$104 and ...:1868$105: identical, with in_l_re_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_im_reg kept as the design's enabled input registers (they are still read by the advance mux and by block2), products and corr_out_tmp removed, latency to corr_out unchanged.

Edit list

  1. corr (:1352-1387): keep the 7-port interface and the corr_out register; the ports now carrying in_l_re/in_r_re receive the advanced values (rename to in_l_re_nxt/in_r_re_nxt). Delete lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg, corr_out_tmp and the always-block arithmetic; instantiate the two chained blocks as in MAPPING; always @(posedge clk) if (new_data) corr_out <= res[15:0];
  2. sh_reg (:1326-1351): add a trailing output dnxt_1 with assign dnxt_1 = wen ? din_1 : dout_1; (a tap on the mux that already feeds dout_1, so no new logic) and extend every positional instantiation in wrapper_corr_20, wrapper_corr_10, wrapper_corr_5_seq with a wire for it (unused in wrapper_corr_5_seq).
  3. wrapper_corr_20 (:1247-1267) and wrapper_corr_10 (:1590-1610): pass inst_sh_reg_k.dnxt_1 and inst_sh_reg_r_1.dnxt_1 in the in_l_re/in_r_re positions of each corr instance; in_l_im/in_r_im keep out_k2/out_r2.
  4. corr_seq (:1829-1871): keep the four input registers and corr_out; delete lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg, corr_out_tmp; instantiate the two chained blocks with ax/ay of block1 driven by new_data ? in_l_re : in_l_re_reg and new_data ? in_r_re : in_r_re_reg.
  5. wrapper_corr_5_seq and everything above corr/corr_seq (the wrappers' corr_out_k registers, the top's 18-bit p+n adders, port_bus_*, the dividers) are untouched.
  6. Do not add a definition of mult_add_int_18x19 to stereovision1.v; instantiate the primitive only.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/.

response

I'll read the design source now.

APPROVE

Verification notes (source checked, not blocking):

  • Arithmetic and timing. corr (stereovision1.v:1371-1386) has in_l_re_reg..in_r_im_reg declared as wires that are never assigned, so the products at :1374-1375 take the ports directly; corr_out_tmp(t) = A(t-2)B(t-2) + C(t-2)D(t-2). With the block model (sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v:39-53), the chained pair gives result2(t) = C(t-2)D(t-2) + A_nxt(t-3)B_nxt(t-3), and A_nxt(t) = A(t+1) because the tap is exactly the wen mux in front of sh_reg.dout_1 (:1338-1349). The two expressions coincide for every wen pattern — no ASSUMES is needed, and none is claimed. corr_seq (:1848-1870) is the same with the enabled input registers in_l_re_reg/in_r_re_reg as the advanced node; its in_l_im_reg/in_r_im_reg feed block2 unadvanced, which is right since the products there read the registered inputs.
  • Taps exist and are correct per instance. inst_corr_k takes out_k1 from inst_sh_reg_k in both wrapper_corr_20 (:1226-1267) and wrapper_corr_10 (:1566-1610), and out_r1 from inst_sh_reg_r_1; wrapper_corr_5_seq (:1727-1739) instantiates corr_seq, so the added sh_reg output is indeed unused there. 2×(21+11)=64 corr, 2×6=12 corr_seq ⇒ 76 regions, 152 blocks, 152 tiles ≤ 306; those are the only four * expressions in the file, so the whole baseline DSP usage is replaced.
  • Visibility. lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg, corr_out_tmp are read only at :1385/:1379 and :1869/:1856 — nothing outside reads them. corr_out keeps its new_data enable outside the block, so no enabled register is pushed into a block.
  • Reset / power-up. None of the replaced registers is cleared by rst, so reset=1'b0 is the behavior-preserving tie-off, including after the testbench's mid-run pulses (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v:184-205). The X window of the 3-deep chain is unreachable: corr_out and the wrapper's corr_out_k load only on vidin_new_data_scld_1/2/4_2to3_left, which is 1 only when counter_out_tmp == 3'b001 (:957-961), and counter_out_tmp is held at 0 by rst through the 4 reset cycles and by zero stimulus afterwards (:937-947), so the first load is far past the third edge.
  • One side remark in the plan is imprecise and does not affect the mapping: rst also clears top-level registers at :437-448 (horiz, vert, video_state, vidin_*_buf_*), and my_wrapper_divider's port named rst is driven by nd, not by the top rst (:1410-1411, :1786-1787). The load-bearing part of the claim — rst never reaches the corr/corr_seq registers — holds.

Read completed /work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v
input
{
 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v"
}
output
1	// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
6	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
7	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
8	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
9	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
10	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
11	// inputs).
12	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
13	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
14	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
15	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
16	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
17	// X until the first clear.
18	module mult_add_int_18x19 (
19	    input clk,
20	    input reset,
21	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
22	    input [17:0] ax,
23	    input [18:0] ay,
24	    input [35:0] bx,
25	    input [63:0] chainin,
26	    input [18:0] scanin,
27	    output reg [63:0] result,
28	    output [63:0] chainout,
29	    output [18:0] scanout
30	);
31	
32	    // stage 1: the input registers
33	    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
34	    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
35	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
36	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
37	
38	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
39	    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
40	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
41	
42	    always @(posedge clk) begin
43	        if (reset) begin
44	            result <= 0;
45	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
46	        end
47	        else begin
48	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
49	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
50	        end
51	    end
52	
53	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
54	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
55	
56	endmodule

prompt
Task

Edit stereovision1.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 306 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Regions in the approved plan: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem). The plan names their shared sum as 'corr_out_tmp of each corr / corr_seq instance (the adder at stereovision1.v:1385 and :1869 that joins the two products of one instance); the further join of the p and n halves is the soft adder in sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem at :316/:368/:400 and stays soft'. In the design their products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh stereovision1.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source and the reference files.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1375$79(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem), corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1868$105(sum_in=sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem)
SHARED_REDUCTION: corr_out_tmp of each corr / corr_seq instance (the adder at stereovision1.v:1385 and :1869 that joins the two products of one instance); the further join of the p and n halves is the soft adder in sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem at :316/:368/:400 and stays soft
BLOCKS: 152
TILES: 152
STAGE_NS: 2.35
MAPPING: per corr instance (64x) and per corr_seq instance (12x), two blocks chained, A/B = the "re" pair, C/D = the "im" pair.
block1 ("re" product, line 1374 / 1867): clk=tm3_clk_v0 (the module's clk port), reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0, A_nxt}, ay={11'b0, B_nxt}, bx=36'b0, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0, result=unconnected, chainout -> block2.chainin, scanout=unconnected.
in corr: A_nxt = the D input of the sh_reg register that produces in_l_re (wen ? d_l_1 : out_01 for instance 0, wen ? out_{k-1}1 : out_k1 for instance k), B_nxt = the D input of the sh_reg register that produces in_r_re (wen ? d_r_1 : out_r1), shared by all corr instances of that wrapper.
in corr_seq: A_nxt = (new_data ? in_l_re : in_l_re_reg), B_nxt = (new_data ? in_r_re : in_r_re_reg).
block2 ("im" product, line 1375 / 1868): clk=same clk, reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0, ax={10'b0, C}, ay={11'b0, D}, bx=36'b0, chainin=block1.chainout, scanin=19'b0, result=res (64 b), chainout=unconnected, scanout=unconnected.
in corr: C = in_l_im (out_k2), D = in_r_im (out_r2), both exactly as today.
in corr_seq: C = in_l_im_reg, D = in_r_im_reg, both exactly as today.
the sum: res[15:0] replaces corr_out_tmp; corr_out stays a soft register: always @(posedge clk) if (new_data) corr_out <= res[15:0];
PLAN:

What is converted and why all four regions together

All four candidate regions are converted; none is left. They are the same arithmetic in two modules: each corr (64 instances) and each corr_seq (12 instances) computes lrexrre_reg = l_re*r_re, limxrim_reg = l_im*r_im, corr_out_tmp = lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg. The baseline's two slowest stages are both of this shape (a 9x9 on a DSP tile straight into a product register, 5.429 / 5.427 ns), and all 152 products are that same shape, so converting a subset leaves a ~5.43 ns stage and buys nothing. The rewrite is therefore all-or-nothing, and it is taken all.

Structure of the rewrite (per corr / corr_seq instance)

Two blocks in a chain replace both products and their adder:

  • block1 computes A*B and exports it on chainout only (its result is unused);
  • block2 computes C*D + chainin, so block2.result = A*B + C*D, which is corr_out_tmp.

Width/sign: all four operands are 8-bit unsigned (input[7:0], no signed), zero-extended into ax/ay; each product fits in 16 bits and the original 16-bit corr_out_tmp wraps mod 2^16, so result[15:0] is bit-identical to the original sum. Every term of the original arithmetic is kept; bx is the only unused port and is tied to 0, as the design has no third addend.

Cycle alignment — why one operand pair is taken one tick early

Writing x(t) for the value during cycle t, the original gives corr_out_tmp(t) = A(t-2)B(t-2) + C(t-2)D(t-2). The chained pair gives result2(t) = ax2(t-2)ay2(t-2) + chainin(t-2) with chainin(t) = chainout1(t) = ax1(t-1)ay1(t-1), i.e. result2(t) = C(t-2)D(t-2) + ax1(t-3)ay1(t-3). Feeding block1 with A_nxt, the D input of the register that holds A (A(t) = A_nxt(t-1)), makes ax1(t-3) = A(t-2) and the two expressions identical, cycle for cycle, with no assumption about wen/new_data. That node already exists: it is the enable mux in front of the sh_reg register (for corr) or in front of in_l_re_reg/in_r_re_reg (for corr_seq), so nothing new is computed, only tapped. There is no ASSUMES: line; the rewrite is exactly equivalent on any stimulus.

corr_out keeps its enable (new_data) and stays a soft register — the block has no clock enable, so no enabled register of the design is moved into a block.

Reset

reset is tied to 1'b0 on all 152 blocks. The registers the blocks replace (lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg, corr_out_tmp) are free-running in the original and are not cleared by the design's rst (rst only reaches port_bus_2to1_1's counter_out_tmp/bus_word_*_tmp, :937-952, port_bus_1to0 and the dividers). Driving the blocks' reset from rst would clear them while the reference keeps counting, and the testbench's mid-run resets (reset_pattern, random rst at 1/64) would then diverge on the first wen after a release; tying it low reproduces the original after every reset. Power-up: with reset low each block loads every edge, so block1's result/chainout are defined from edge 1 and block2's result from edge 3. That X window cannot be captured: corr_out only loads when vidin_new_data_scld_1_2to3_left (etc.) is 1, and that signal is a register of counter_out_tmp, which rst holds at 0 through the testbench's 4 reset cycles, so the earliest load is well after cycle 3. Nothing relies on the initial value of any added register (none is added).

Blocks, tiles, area

152 blocks (2 per converted instance x 76 instances), each alone on a DSP tile: the design then uses 152 DSP tiles of the 306 available, against 42 in the baseline (the 152 mult_9x9_fixed_pt products, four to a tile, all disappear). DSP area goes 1.066e7 -> 3.857e7. Against that, 48 flip-flops (lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg, corr_out_tmp) and one 16-bit adder leave soft logic per instance — 3648 FFs and ~1216 LUTs, roughly a third of the design's ~536 CLBs, about 4.9e6 of CLB area. Estimated new area ~4.86e7, i.e. about 1.9x the baseline; break-even on area x delay is therefore a period of ~2.89 ns against the baseline's 5.47292. This is the cost the plan deliberately accepts: the block is 4x less area-efficient per multiply than the fractured 9x9, so the rewrite only pays if it roughly halves the period, which it does for every stage it touches.

Stages

  • Longest series of blocks in one stage: 2 (block1's input register -> chainout -> block2's chainin register), delay 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns — the dedicated chain costs exactly the same as a block's own stage, so chaining is free.
  • Block internal stage (input registers -> result): 0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219 ns.
  • Into a block: soft FF 0.060 + wire 1.417-1.940 (median 1.732) + setup 0.019 = 1.81-2.02 ns; for block1 the advance mux adds one LUT on that path, ~2.3-2.4 ns — this is the slowest stage the rewrite leaves, ~2.35 ns.
  • Out of a block: result clock-to-Q 0.060 + wire 0.933-1.729 (median 1.314) + the new_data enable mux + FF setup, ~1.5-2.0 ns.

Soft logic leaving the slowest stage: the baseline's slowest stage and its twin (both register -> wire 1.73 -> mult_9x9 -> wire 1.45 -> product FF, 5.429 / 5.427 ns) are removed outright — the multiply now sits between two registers inside the block and the two wires become separate stages. The 16-bit adder and its register (corr_out_tmp) also leave their stage, which was not the slowest. Nothing is added to any stage except the one LUT of the advance mux noted above.

Caveat stated plainly: the two slowest stages on the seed-1 report are both removed, and the report gives nothing below them, so the resulting period is set by untouched soft stages I cannot read off what I was given — the likely candidates are the 18-bit corr_out_1X/2X/4X adders in sv_chip1_hierarchy_no_mem (:316, :368, :400) and the 17-bit adder in wrapper_norm (:1445). If those sit near 2.4-2.9 ns the rewrite wins by up to ~19%; above ~2.9 ns it loses. That is the measurement this candidate is worth making.

Register stages, operands to result, for each converted region

  • corr.$mul$...:1374$78 and ...:1375$79: before — operands (out_k1/out_k2/out_r1/out_r2) -> 1 stage -> product register -> 1 more stage -> corr_out_tmp: 2 stages to the sum. After — block1 operands are the same values taken one shift-tick early, then block1 input registers (1) -> block2 chainin register (2) -> block2 result (3): 3 block stages, but the one-tick-early tap cancels one, so the sum still lands exactly 2 stages after the design's own registered operands, on the same cycle as corr_out_tmp did. corr_out is unchanged, 1 further enabled stage.
  • corr_seq.$mul$...:1867$104 and ...:1868$105: identical, with in_l_re_reg/in_r_re_reg/in_l_im_reg/in_r_im_reg kept as the design's enabled input registers (they are still read by the advance mux and by block2), products and corr_out_tmp removed, latency to corr_out unchanged.

Edit list

  1. corr (:1352-1387): keep the 7-port interface and the corr_out register; the ports now carrying in_l_re/in_r_re receive the advanced values (rename to in_l_re_nxt/in_r_re_nxt). Delete lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg, corr_out_tmp and the always-block arithmetic; instantiate the two chained blocks as in MAPPING; always @(posedge clk) if (new_data) corr_out <= res[15:0];
  2. sh_reg (:1326-1351): add a trailing output dnxt_1 with assign dnxt_1 = wen ? din_1 : dout_1; (a tap on the mux that already feeds dout_1, so no new logic) and extend every positional instantiation in wrapper_corr_20, wrapper_corr_10, wrapper_corr_5_seq with a wire for it (unused in wrapper_corr_5_seq).
  3. wrapper_corr_20 (:1247-1267) and wrapper_corr_10 (:1590-1610): pass inst_sh_reg_k.dnxt_1 and inst_sh_reg_r_1.dnxt_1 in the in_l_re/in_r_re positions of each corr instance; in_l_im/in_r_im keep out_k2/out_r2.
  4. corr_seq (:1829-1871): keep the four input registers and corr_out; delete lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg, corr_out_tmp; instantiate the two chained blocks with ax/ay of block1 driven by new_data ? in_l_re : in_l_re_reg and new_data ? in_r_re : in_r_re_reg.
  5. wrapper_corr_5_seq and everything above corr/corr_seq (the wrappers' corr_out_k registers, the top's 18-bit p+n adders, port_bus_*, the dividers) are untouched.
  6. Do not add a definition of mult_add_int_18x19 to stereovision1.v; instantiate the primitive only.
Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output on every cycle after the testbench's reset phase (its first 4 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_sv1_top.v) and the block's behavioural model (mult_add_int_18x19.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

What I changed, region by region

corr — regions corr.$mul$stereovision1.v:1374$78 and :1375$79 (stereovision1.v:1364)
Deleted lrexrre_reg, limxrim_reg, corr_out_tmp and the whole arithmetic always-block. In their place, two chained blocks:

  • mult_re: ax={10'b0,in_l_re_nxt}, ay={11'b0,in_r_re_nxt}, bx=36'b0, chainin=64'b0, scanin=19'b0, reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0; result unconnected, chainout → mult_im.chainin, scanout unconnected.
  • mult_im: ax={10'b0,in_l_im}, ay={11'b0,in_r_im}, bx=36'b0, chainin=mult_re.chainout, same tie-offs; result = corr_res, chainout/scanout unconnected.

The two "re" ports were renamed in_l_re_nxt / in_r_re_nxt: they now carry the value one tick early, which cancels the extra chain stage. corr_out stays a soft register with its new_data enable, loading corr_res[15:0].

corr_seq — regions corr_seq.$mul$stereovision1.v:1867$104 and :1868$105 (stereovision1.v:1868)
Same two chained blocks. The four enabled input registers stay soft. mult_re is fed by the new wires in_l_re_nxt/in_r_re_nxt = new_data ? in_l_re : in_l_re_reg (the D input of those registers, tapped, not new logic); mult_im is fed by in_l_im_reg/in_r_im_reg unchanged. corr_out keeps its new_data enable and loads corr_res[15:0].

sh_reg (stereovision1.v:1349)
Added a trailing output dnxt_1 with assign dnxt_1 = (wen == 1'b1) ? din_1 : dout_1; — a tap on the mux that already feeds dout_1.

wrapper_corr_20 (:1225) and wrapper_corr_10 (:1605)
Added the dnxt_*1 wires, extended each sh_reg instantiation with its dnxt_1, and moved each corr instance's in_l_re/in_r_re arguments from out_k1/out_r1 to dnxt_k1/dnxt_r1. in_l_im/in_r_im still take out_k2/out_r2.

wrapper_corr_5_seq (:1779)
Only the sh_reg instantiations were extended with unused dnxt_*1 wires; the corr_seq instances are unchanged (the advance happens inside corr_seq).

Everything above corr/corr_seq — the wrappers' corr_out_k registers, the top's 18-bit p+n adders at :316/:368/:400, port_bus_*, the dividers — is untouched.

Reset and verification

All 152 blocks have reset tied to 1'b0, matching the free-running registers they replace (the design's rst never reached them).

No ASSUMES: line is needed — the one-tick-early tap makes the rewrite equivalent on any stimulus, with no condition on wen/new_data.

Checks run:

  • A focused Icarus testbench (20 000 cycles, random data and random wen) comparing the rewritten sh_reg+corr/corr_seq against a copy of the original modules, at two taps of the shift chain: 80 000 comparisons, 0 mismatches.
  • A whole-design run of the reference testbench's stimulus (reset phase, lead-in, impulse probe, reset pattern, 6000 random cycles with mid-run resets) with the edited design against a reconstructed original: 0 mismatches on all 12 outputs over 6121 compared cycles, and 0 cycles with X on the DUT only — the block's 3-cycle power-up X window never reaches an output, because new_data is held low for ~20 cycles by the rst-held counter_out_tmp. Note this run used Icarus' X initialization rather than the harness' $init_zero (the VPI task is not available here), which is why the reference itself shows X on some cycles.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true

// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
// inputs).
// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
// X until the first clear.
module mult_add_int_18x19 (
    input clk,
    input reset,
    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
    input [17:0] ax,
    input [18:0] ay,
    input [35:0] bx,
    input [63:0] chainin,
    input [18:0] scanin,
    output reg [63:0] result,
    output [63:0] chainout,
    output [18:0] scanout
);

    // stage 1: the input registers
    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;

    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64

    always @(posedge clk) begin
        if (reset) begin
            result <= 0;
            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
        end
        else begin
            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
        end
    end

    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
    assign scanout  = ay_reg;

endmodule
====

module corr_seq (clk, new_data, in_l_re, in_l_im, in_r_re, in_r_im, corr_out);

    parameter sh_reg_w  = 4'b1000;
    input clk; 
    input new_data; 
    input[sh_reg_w - 1:0] in_l_re; 
    input[sh_reg_w - 1:0] in_l_im; 
    input[sh_reg_w - 1:0] in_r_re; 
    input[sh_reg_w - 1:0] in_r_im; 
    output[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] corr_out; 
    reg[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] corr_out;
    reg[sh_reg_w - 1:0] in_l_re_reg; 
    reg[sh_reg_w - 1:0] in_l_im_reg; 
    reg[sh_reg_w - 1:0] in_r_re_reg; 
    reg[sh_reg_w - 1:0] in_r_im_reg; 
    reg[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] lrexrre_reg; 
    reg[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] limxrim_reg; 
    reg[2 * sh_reg_w - 1:0] corr_out_tmp; 

    always @(posedge clk)
    begin
          if (new_data == 1'b1)
          begin
             in_l_re_reg <= in_l_re ; 
             in_l_im_reg <= in_l_im ; 
             in_r_re_reg <= in_r_re ; 
             in_r_im_reg <= in_r_im ; 
             corr_out <= corr_out_tmp ; 
          end 
          else
          begin
             in_l_re_reg <= in_l_re_reg ; 
             in_l_im_reg <= in_l_im_reg ; 
             in_r_re_reg <= in_r_re_reg ; 
             in_r_im_reg <= in_r_im_reg ; 
             corr_out <= corr_out; 
          end 
			// PAJ - replaced by me, but called mult_slow
             lrexrre_reg <= in_l_re_reg*in_r_re_reg ; 
             limxrim_reg <= in_l_im_reg*in_r_im_reg ; 
          corr_out_tmp <= lrexrre_reg + limxrim_reg ; 
    end 
 endmodule

{
 "file_path": "/tmp/fix/patch_wrappers.py",
 "content": "import re, sys\n\np = \"/work/stereovision1.v\"\nsrc = open(p).read()\nlines = src.split(\"\\n\")\n\n# find module boundaries\ndef module_range(name):\n    start = None\n    for i, l in enumerate(lines):\n        if re.match(r\"\\s*module\\s+%s\\s*\\(\" % re.escape(name), l):\n            start = i\n            break\n    assert start is not None, name\n    for j in range(start, len(lines)):\n        if lines[j].strip() == \"endmodule\":\n            return start, j\n    raise AssertionError(name)\n\nsh_re = re.compile(r\"^(\\s*sh_reg\\s+inst_sh_reg_\\S+?)\\((.*?)\\);(\\s*)$\")\ncorr_re = re.compile(r\"^(\\s*corr\\s+inst_corr_\\S+?)\\((.*?)\\);(\\s*)$\")\n\nfor mod, patch_corr in ((\"wrapper_corr_20\", True),\n                        (\"wrapper_corr_10\", True),\n                        (\"wrapper_corr_5_seq\", False)):\n    a, b = module_range(mod)\n    dnxt_names = []\n    first_sh = None\n    for i in range(a, b):\n        m = sh_re.match(lines[i])\n        if m:\n            if first_sh is None:\n                first_sh = i\n            args = [x.strip() for x in m.group(2).split(\",\")]\n            assert len(args) == 6, (mod, lines[i])\n            out1 = args[4]\n            assert out1.startswith(\"out_\") and out1.endswith(\"1\"), out1\n            dn = \"dnxt_\" + out1[len(\"out_\"):]\n            dnxt_names.append(dn)\n            lines[i] = \"%s(%s, %s); %s\" % (m.group(1), \", \".join(args), dn, m.group(3).strip())\n            continue\n        if patch_corr:\n            m = corr_re.match(lines[i])\n            if m:\n                args = [x.strip() for x in m.group(2).split(\",\")]\n                assert len(args) == 7, (mod, lines[i])\n                for k in (2, 4):  # in_l_re, in_r_re positions\n                    assert args[k].startswith(\"out_\") and args[k].endswith(\"1\"), args[k]\n                    args[k] = \"dnxt_\" + args[k][len(\"out_\"):]\n                lines[i] = \"%s(%s); %s\" % (m.group(1), \", \".join(args), m.group(3).strip())\n    assert first_sh is not None, mod\n    decls = [\"   wire[sh_reg_w - 1:0] %s; \" % d for d in dnxt_names] + [\"\"]\n    lines[first_sh:first_sh] = decls\n    print(mod, len(dnxt_names), \"dnxt wires\")\n\nopen(p, \"w\").write(\"\\n\".join(lines))\n"
}

begin p = p ^ (p<<13); p = p ^ (p>>17); p = p ^ (p<<5); end endtask\n  task cmp; input [8*8-1:0] nm; input [15:0] e, a; begin\n    checked = checked + 1;\n    if (e !== a) begin\n      errors = errors + 1;\n      if (errors < 12) $display(\"MISMATCH cyc=%0d %0s ref=%h dut=%h\", cyc, nm, e, a);\n    end else if (e !== 0) nz = nz + 1;\n  end endtask\n\n  initial begin\n    wen = 0; dl1 = 0; dl2 = 0; dr1 = 0; dr2 = 0;\n    // the design holds new_data low for well over 3 cycles after power-up (see the\n    // counter_out_tmp / vidin_new_data path); mirror that here.\n    repeat (6) begin @(negedge clk); cyc = cyc + 1;\n      draw; dl1 = p[7:0]; draw; dl2 = p[7:0]; draw; dr1 = p[7:0]; draw; dr2 = p[7:0]; end\n    for (i = 0; i < 20000; i = i + 1) begin\n      @(negedge clk); cyc = cyc + 1;\n      cmp(\"corr0\", ref_c0, dut_c0);\n      cmp(\"corr2\", ref_c2, dut_c2);\n      cmp(\"cseq0\", ref_s0, dut_s0);\n      cmp(\"cseq2\", ref_s2, dut_s2);\n      draw; dl1 = p[7:0]; draw; dl2 = p[7:0]; draw; dr1 = p[7:0]; draw; dr2 = p[7:0];\n      draw; wen = (p[3:0] < 10);   // ~62% duty, long runs of both values\n    end\n    $display(\"checked=%0d nonzero=%0d errors=%0d\", checked, nz, errors);\n    if (errors == 0) $display(\"FOCUSED EQUIVALENCE: PASS\"); else $display(\"FOCUSED EQUIVALENCE: FAIL\");\n    $finish;\n  end\nendmodule\n"
